FOR TEACHERS · CONTINUED

How the book is built, and why

✎For teachers

Every unit has four teaching parts. Each part carries a worked example, at least
twelve practice items, a reasoning prompt headed How do you know, and a second-
method panel headed Check it a different way. Those four are enforced by automated
gates at build time rather than by editorial good intentions, so a part cannot ship
without them.

Body type is set at 14.5 point and is never reduced to make a page fit. Where content
overruns, the page is split. This follows the acceptance criterion agreed after the
Year 7 review and applies across the whole Prime Books line.

No answer is printed anywhere in this book except inside a Watch me work one out
panel, where printing it is the point. The Check your progress items at the end of each
unit are deliberately different from the practice items, so that the check measures
learning rather than recall of drilled questions.

The self-assessment tables are worded confident, almost there and needs more work.
Colour words are not used, so that a pupil who cannot distinguish red from green is
not disadvantaged and so that the record reads as a sentence to a parent.

Pupils who finish a part early should be directed to Take it further, then to the Yard
challenge, and then to the unit's QR activity, rather than into the next unit. The
projects are designed to absorb a wide range of finishing speeds inside one class.

Every QR code prints its address as readable text. No activity in this book requires a
device, and nothing is lost by a class that never scans one.

SOURCES AND REFERENCES · CONTINUED

The curriculum framework

Cambridge Assessment International Education, Cambridge Primary Mathematics 0845 Curriculum
Framework, Stage 2. Published by the University of Cambridge Local Examinations Syndicate. The
objective codes quoted throughout this book (2Nn, 2Nc, 2Gs, 2Gp, 2Mm, 2Ml, 2Mt, 2Dh and 2Pt) are
the framework's own codes and are quoted for identification and planning only.

The interactive resources

**Mathigon Polypad, mathigon.org/polypad. A free collection of virtual manipulatives, used in this book**
wherever a printed figure needed to move: number lines, arrays, fraction bars, coins, clocks, polygons
and polyhedra. Mathigon is part of Amplify Education, Inc.

**NRICH, nrich.maths.org. The primary problem collection of the Millennium Mathematics Project at the**
University of Cambridge, used for the open-ended problems that extend the projects.

**Topmarks, www.topmarks.co.uk. Free browser-based practice games needing no account, used where**
the value of the link was a fresh set of questions every time.

Typefaces

Inter by Rasmus Andersson. Andika by SIL International. Kalam by Indian Type Foundry. Lexend by
Thomas Jockin and Bonnie Shaver-Troup. All are used under the SIL Open Font License 1.1.

A note on the mathematics

Every number, total, array, coin total, measurement and clock face printed in this book is computed by
the typesetting engine from the underlying data, not typed by hand. A printed total therefore cannot
disagree with the picture beside it. The engine's arithmetic is proved by 306 self-test assertions that
run before every build.

**P R I M E  B O O K S**

## Mathematics

Year 2 · Cambridge Early Years · Student Manual

Draw it. Work it out. Then check it a different way.

Last year you learned to count something, and then to count it again to be sure.

This book asks for the next thing, which is the thing that makes a mathematician: a

method, and then a second, different method that agrees with the first.

Otto draws the plan. Rook lines everything into rows and columns. Bramble keeps

the store, the scales and the money tin. And Tessa checks every single thing a

different way, which is why this book exists.

**INSIDE THIS BOOK**

•
Twenty-one units covering the full Cambridge Primary

Mathematics Stage 2 framework, with the objective codes
printed on every unit
•
Over a thousand practice items, and not one printed answer to

copy
•
A worked example, a reasoning prompt and a second method in

every part
•
Seven Build it and show it projects, with the judging grid

printed before the work starts
•
QR codes that only appear where the screen can do something

the page cannot

**Prime Books · Mathematics**

Ages 6–7 · Lower Primary
